Staff have not operated out of the facility for almost a year because of safety reasons , but Israeli forces confiscated devices and forced out the private security guards hired to protect the facility . <p> " What we saw today is the culmination of two years of incitement and measures against UNRWA in east Jerusalem , " Friedrich said , calling it a violation of international law guaranteeing such facilities protection . <p> He said forces also began firing tear gas at the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ on Tuesday afternoon . More than 300 young refugees receive job training in technology and welding there . <p> Some children on their way home from the school were overcome by the tear gas and a 15-year-old was hit in the eye with a rubber bullet , according to the Palestinian Authority 's Jerusalem governate , which monitors Palestinian affairs in the area . <p> Israeli leaders celebrate demolition <p> Israel 's Foreign Ministry said the demolition enforced a new law banning UNRWA , noting that Israel owns the site and rejecting UNRWA 's claims that the move violated international law . <p> Israel has long claimed the agency has an anti-Israel bias , often with little evidence . It says UNRWA employs and maintains ties with militant groups including Hamas . The U.N . has ardently denied such claims and UNRWA has said it acts quickly to purge any suspected militants among its staff . <p> UNRWA 's mandate is to provide aid and services to some 2.5 million Palestinian refugees in Gaza , the occupied West Bank and east Jerusalem , as well as 3 million more refugees @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ for years maintained infrastructure in refugee camps and also run schools and provide health care . But its operations were curtailed last year when Israel 's Knesset passed legislation severing ties and banning it from functioning in what it defines as Israel -- including east Jerusalem . <p> The agency said the demolitions could imperil operations at its vocational center in Qalandia and heath facility in Shua'fat , where it continues to provide education and health services . <p> An Israeli flag was seen hoisted above the facility in the Sheikh Jarrah neighborhood , where some Israeli politicians arrived on the scene to celebrate the organization 's fate . National Security Minister Itamar Ben Gvir called it " a historic day . " <p> ' This must be a wake-up call ' <p> The demolition marked the culmination of years of criticism from Israel and its leaders , who contend that UNRWA harbors pro-Palestinian leanings and maintains ties or employees members of militant groups like Hamas . <p> Since the start of the Israel-Hamas war more than two years ago , it has ramped up such attacks , accusing UNRWA @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ its facilities and seized aid . It has provided little evidence for the claims , which the U.N . has denied . The International Court of Justice said in October that Israel must allow the agency to provide humanitarian assistance in Gaza . <p> Since Israel passed the law banning the agency last year , its facilities -- schools and health centers -- and its headquarters have repeatedly been closed , raided or left unprotected . Israel has contended the agency perpetuates Palestinians ' refugee status , while UNRWA supporters have said Israel 's attacks on the agency are aimed at sidelining the issue -- one of the most contentious dividing Israelis and Palestinians . <p> " This comes in the wake of other steps taken by Israeli authorities to erase the Palestine Refugee identity , " Philippe Lazzarini , UNRWA 's commissioner-general , said in a statement on X. " This must be a wake-up call . What happens today to UNRWA will happen tomorrow to any other international organisation or diplomatic mission , whether in the Occupied Palestinian Territory or anywhere around the world . " <p> Register @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ United States cut funding for the agency in 2018 . President Joe Biden restored it in 2021 and later paused funding in 2024 . <p> Israel 's ban on UNRWA dovetailed with broader efforts to deregister aid groups operating in Gaza and the occupied West Bank . Israel has passed laws requiring nongovernmental organizations not to hire staff involved in activities that " delegitimize Israel " or support boycotts , demanding they register lists of names as a condition of being allowed to work . <p> Israel told dozens of groups -- including Doctors Without Borders and CARE -- that their licenses would expire at the end of 2025 . The organizations say the rules are arbitrary and warned that the new ban would harm a civilian population desperately in need of humanitarian aid .

For more information about the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ DAVOS -- Prime Minister Mark Carney says Canada and other middle power countries must stop pandering to unnamed " great powers " that are upending global trade and territorial boundaries , and form new strategic alliances to protect their interests , citizens , and values . <p> In a hard-hitting and wide-ranging speech that nevertheless did not name U.S. President Donald Trump but made reference to " American hegemony , " Carney set out the basis of Canada 's foreign policy in the face of a crumbling rules-based international order . <p> Prime Minister Mark Carney delivers remarks and participates in a discussion while attending a meeting of the World Economic Forum ( WEF ) in Davos , Switzerland . <p> " Values-based realism " will form the basis of Canada 's approach , said Carney , guided by adherence to basic principles and accepting that there will not always be complete alignment among those nations that must form new alliances to survive an era where great powers are " using economic integration as weapons , tariffs as leverage , financial infrastructure as coercion , and supply chains as vulnerabilities @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ It was as clear a broadside at the U.S. president and his " America First " policies as any Carney has delivered since last spring 's election campaign , when his " elbows up " rhetoric was spicier , more challenging and critical of Trump than it has been since the Liberal leader took office amid a trade war . <p> One American political scientist has urged European leaders not to back down or make concessions to Trump -- certainly not by giving on Greenland . <p> " We aim to be principled and pragmatic , " said Carney . " Principled in our commitment to fundamental values : sovereignty and territorial integrity , the prohibition of the use of force except when consistent with the UN Charter , respect for human rights . " <p> " Pragmatic in recognizing that progress is often incremental , that interests diverge , that not every partner shares our values . We are engaging broadly , strategically , with open eyes . " <p> How exactly Carney intends to apply this approach when it comes to challenging Trump on Greenland is not clear @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Carney reiterated that when it comes to Arctic sovereignty , Canada stands " firmly with Greenland and Denmark and fully support their unique right to determine Greenland 's future , " and is " unwavering " in its commitment to NATO 's mutual defence pledge that an attack on one is an attack on all . <p> He said Canada is working with NATO allies and eight Nordic and Baltic nations " to further secure the alliance 's northern and western flanks , including through unprecedented investments in over-the-horizon radar , submarines , aircraft , and boots on the ground . " <p> But neither Carney nor his officials clarified exactly what actions Ottawa is prepared to take to defend Greenland and Danish sovereignty in the region , although the Star and other outlets have reported he is considering sending troops there . <p> And Carney did not outright criticize Trump as the U.S. president ramped up overnight his rhetoric around his plan to take control of Greenland . <p> Trump has already threatened tariffs against eight European nations who voiced opposition to his taking over Greenland , including Denmark @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ United Kingdom , the Netherlands and Finland . <p> Trump arrives in Davos in the wee hours of Wednesday and it is not clear whether Carney will meet with him . <p> Trump has scheduled meetings with foreign leaders for Wednesday , and on Thursday will make an announcement here about his Board of Peace charter . <p> Carney 's government is now signalling that the Canadian prime minister is reconsidering whether to join Trump 's Board of Peace to oversee the reconstruction of Gaza . <p> Carney said on Saturday he 'd accepted Trump 's invitation " in principle . " That was before Trump publicly confirmed that he has invited Russian President Vladimir Putin to join the Board of Peace . <p> Finance Minister Fran ? ois-Philippe Champagne told reporters here that a final decision had not yet been made , as he said Canada would not pay the $1 billion price tag that Trump is reportedly setting to join the board as a permanent member . Officials had earlier said Canada was not asked to pay any entry fee . <p> " The prime minister will have @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ known and all the details have been hammered out , whether this is in the best interest of Canada to be part of the process , " Champagne said . <p> Carney said then that Canada will do business with countries like China where it makes sense . <p> On Tuesday here he said Canada will take on the world " as it is , not wait for the world as we wish it to be . " <p> Middle countries must forget the " fiction " that the rules-based international order can , or ever did , really protect them and stop accommodating great powers which are bullying the rest of the world , in a speech that was peppered with references to Thucydides , Finnish President Alexander Stubb and former Czech dissident V ? clav Havel , who went on to become president . <p> " We knew the story of the international rules-based order was partially false . That the strongest would exempt themselves when convenient , " said Carney . " This fiction was useful , and American hegemony , in particular , helped provide public @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , collective security , and support for frameworks for resolving disputes . " <p> But the last two decades , which have seen a series of crises in finance , health , energy , and geopolitics , " laid bare the risks of extreme global integration , " said Carney , and the " bargain no longer works . @@133592823 <p> The Art Gallery of Ontario is reckoning with the resignations of a senior curator and two volunteer members of a collections committee after the group narrowly voted to not acquire a work by Nan Goldin over accusations that the Jewish-American photographer 's views are antisemitic . <p> Ms. Goldin , a celebrated photographer and a long-time activist , used a late 2024 speech at Berlin 's Neue Nationalgalerie to share her " moral outrage at the genocide in Gaza and Lebanon . " She levelled criticism at Israel for the tens of thousands of deaths reported since it launched its war on Hamas in 2023 , after the group 's Oct. 7 attacks that left 1,200 dead in Israel and 251 others taken as hostages . <p> Months after her remarks , the Toronto gallery set out to jointly purchase the American artist 's video work Stendhal Syndrome with the Vancouver Art Gallery and Minneapolis 's Walker Art Center . <p> But the AGO stepped back from the joint acquisition in the middle of last year , after its modern and contemporary curatorial working committee voted 11-9 against it @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the artist 's remarks were " offensive " and " antisemitic , " according to an internal memo from AGOdirector and chief executive Stephan Jost that was obtained by The Globe and Mail . <p> The committee members who voted to acquire Ms. Goldin 's work felt that the remarks were not antisemitic , with some adding that " refusing the work because of the artist 's views was censorship , " according to the memo . <p> The AGO 's modern and contemporary curator , John Zeppetelli , who had advocated for the acquisition , eventually resigned from that full-time position in connection with the incident , two people with direct knowledge of the situation said . <p> Two members of the modern and contemporary collections committee subsequently resigned their volunteer roles as well , according to the sources . Their decisions were also directly tied to the debate over and decision not to acquire Stendhal Syndrome , one source added . The Globe is not identifying the sources because they were not authorized to publicly discuss the situation . <p> The decision happened even as the AGO , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ houses three of Ms. Goldin 's works . The gallery is publicly funded , receiving $26-million from governments in its last fiscal year , primarily from Ontario . <p> Mr. Zeppetelli is still working with the AGO for the next few months in a reduced capacity as a guest curator , co-organizingan exhibition of work from the Italian artist Diego Marcon that is scheduled to open in June . He did not respond to repeated requests for comment . <p> In his memo , Mr. Jost outlined a governance review that followed the vote . The review recommended a " reset " on the committee 's acquisition discussions , and " clarification " of its members ' responsibilities . <p> In an e-mailed statement , the AGO acknowledged both the discord over Ms. Goldin 's work and the governance review . " Personal political views were brought into the conversation . This is not intended to be part of the process , " spokesperson Laura Quinn said . <p> " Our reset is to ensure that conversations remain focused on an artwork 's alignment to the AGO 's acquisition criteria @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . " <p> Stendhal Syndrome does not touch upon the politics of Ms. Goldin 's speech in Berlin , but rather juxtaposes her photos of classical , Renaissance and baroque artworks with portraits of her loved ones . The title references the intense psychosomatic response some people feel when viewing a striking piece of art . Neither Ms. Goldin nor Gagosian , the major global gallery that represents her , responded to requests for comment . <p> Mr. Zeppetelli joined the AGO as its curator of modern and contemporary art in September , 2024 , after leading the Mus ? e d'art contemporain de Montr ? al . He pitched acquiring Ms. Goldin 's work to the modern and contemporary collections committee within months of joining . <p> When the committee of about 20 people -- largely from around Toronto and connected to the art world -- convened for a May , 2025 , meeting to discuss acquisitions , Ms. Goldin 's was one of five works from the 20th and 21st centuries under consideration . Though the committee had limited funding and could not acquire all five , discussion @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 's politics . <p> While members of the committee largely agreed that Stendhal Syndrome had artistic merit , they voted against acquiring it , and instead recommended a different work for acquisition . <p> Several members of the committee then pressed Mr. Jost to acquire the work through a separate tranche of funding , according to a source . But the request went unheeded . The Vancouver Art Gallery and Walker Art Center went on to acquire Stendhal Syndrome without the AGO 's participation ; it has been on display in Vancouver since November . <p> Zainub Verjee , the executive director of Galeries Ontario / Ontario Galleries , an advocacy group serving public art galleries , declined to comment on the AGO situation specifically -- but said collections are n't meant to be " a comfort zone . " <p> Instead , she said , collections should be " a record of what a society dared to look at . " <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ acquisitions are influenced by political litmus tests or opaque committee dynamics , it can have a chilling effect on trust and willingness to engage with the institution . " <p> Stephen Borys , who launched the arts-consulting firm Civic Muse after running the Winnipeg Art Gallery and its Inuit art centre Qaumajuq for nearly two decades , said : " There is always a danger when an acquisition is made that would appear to be based on an artist 's political perspective or comments . " <p> This , he said , could lead to tumult for the curator and the artist , " but also for artistic expression , the institution 's credibility and public trust . " He also only spoke in general terms , and not about the AGO 's Stendhal Syndrome vote . @@133592839 <p> Katherine Blouin is an associate professor of history and classics at the University of Toronto . Matthew A. Sears is professor of classics at the University of New Brunswick . <p> On Jan. 20 , Prime Minister Mark Carney gave a surprisingly candid speech at the 2026 World Economic Forum in Davos , insisting that the " rules-based international order " is a fiction on which we can no longer rely . His speech came after U.S. President Donald Trump posted an altered picture showing a map where Canada , Venezuela and Greenland are covered with an American flag . <p> To set up his speech , framed by V ? clav Havel 's essay The Power of the Powerless , Mr. Carney quoted a famous line from the ancient Greek historian and general Thucydides : " The strong do what they can ; the weak suffer what they must . " This kind of lawless might-is-right approach to international relations , Mr. Carney argued , is precisely what we can no longer afford . <p> This aphorism , long championed by realist political theorists , comes from Thucydides @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and relatively powerless island of Melos debate their freedom with imperial Athens -- a hegemon , as Mr. Carney would say . Amid the Peloponnesian War between the Athenians and the Spartans , the Melians were given a choice : either surrender and pay tribute , or be destroyed . In 416 BC , following the Melians ' refusal to surrender , Athenian forces besieged the island , killed all military-aged men , sold the women and children into slavery , and gave the land over to Athenian settlers . <p> In his History of the Peloponnesian War , Thucydides reimagined the dialogue between the Athenians and Melians that preceded the siege . The Melians appeal to justice , while the Athenians respond with the line quoted by Mr. Carney . The Melians ' reply , which the Prime Minister did not cite , is nevertheless hinted at in the rest of his speech : " In our opinion it is in your interest to maintain a principle which is for the good of all -- that anyone in danger should have just and equitable treatment ... This is in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ would involve you in a crushing punishment that would be a lesson to the world . " <p> Given the fate of Melos , it really might seem as though justice has no place in international relations -- that only power determines things . Yet , after becoming hated by virtually all the Greeks and watching ally after ally revolt from its brutal hegemony , Athens lost the war . Thucydides wrote the Melian Dialogue with this end in mind , just as Mr. Carney delivered his speech with an apparent eye to the inevitability of the collapse of the American empire . Far from presenting justice and expediency as opposites , with expediency winning out , Thucydides means for his readers to conceive of justice as expedient . <p> Mr. Carney 's speech focuses on the rupture of the " rules-based order " as it applies to the relationship between what he calls " middle powers " and the American hegemon ( which he never names explicitly ) . Yet , as Mr. Carney himself hints , these same middle powers have for decades happily acted as vassals of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ only maintained the illusion of the rules-based order through ritualistic " performances of sovereignty " that are now backfiring ; they have also enabled a plethora of U.S.-led Melian tragedies worldwide , including in Gaza and Venezuela . <p> Just as the hubris that characterizes the Athenians ' horrific treatment of the Melians foreshadowed their own eventual fall against the Spartans , so is America 's open dismemberment of the rules-based order symptomatic of its unfolding collapse . When Mr. Carney , an expert on economic risk management , speaks of a " rupture , " he refers not only to the tearing of the veil of democracy , equality and freedom that has been used to sugar-coat American imperialism for decades , but also to the more-than-precarious position of the American economy , which tariffs and dedollarization might only worsen . <p> Though resistance might have appeared futile and foolish at the height of Athenian power , and the people of Melos certainly paid a high price , that was not the conclusion of the story . In the end , the Athenians suffered the consequences of the brutal @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ thing , and imperial hubris a self-sabotaging curse , even the greatest powers are eventually eclipsed or overthrown . Treating everyone justly , including ( or especially ? ) the less powerful , is not only the right thing to do ; it is also , in the long run , in the interests of all parties . We need true justice in international relations -- for the sake of our humanity , but also to enjoy its protections when we find ourselves at the wrong end of power relations . 
@@133592850 <p> Evan Fraser is director of the Arrell Food Institute at the University of Guelph . <p> Lenore Newman is director of the Food and Agriculture Institute at the University of Fraser Valley . <p> In Davos on Tuesday , Prime Minister Mark Carney said the quiet part out loud : " A country that can not feed itself , fuel itself or defend itself has few options . When the rules no longer protect you , you must protect yourself . " <p> How right he is , and we all should worry . <p> A huge proportion of the fruits and vegetables eaten by Canadians travel across international borders , and most of this is either produced in the United States or transits through a U.S. port or along an American highway . An irascible U.S. government could starve us within days , and we would have no easy alternatives . So , as old alliances continue to fray , we must realize Greenland , Venezuela , Ukraine and Gaza are wake-up calls . We are living in a world of sharks who do n't think twice about @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are also racing against time in terms of climate change . By the end of the century , huge areas in the Southern U.S. , as well as in Latin America , Africa and Asia , will become less productive . In some places , agriculture may fail entirely . <p> Climate and geopolitical turbulence mean we must now question whether we will be able to continue importing the food we need . <p> Recent work we 've done suggests that this stark predicament presents a huge opportunity for Canada . As global temperatures rise , and coastal regions are inundated , there will be a shift in where agricultural land is located . People farming around the equator will lose out , but Canada stands to gain more than any country except Russia . In addition , our country has more fresh water than anywhere else on the planet.Canada is on track to become the world 's most important food-producing region by 2100 . <p> In a fractured world where would-be emperors dream of hemispheric dominance , does this mean we have a target painted on our collective backs @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ where wheat and canola replace critical minerals or fossil fuels ? Or will we emerge as a critical linchpin , strategically located between major powers ? <p> It is time to be bold and recognize that Canada can -- and must -- become the world 's most important food-producing region in the latter part of the 21st century . Our role as global breadbasket can position us for success in this most volatile of centuries . If we do n't act , we leave our fate for others to determine . <p> One of the challenges in acting on the vision is a lack of understanding or interest in what most see as the unexciting work of feeding a nation . It 's only when things go wrong ( often because of a food-safety issue ) that agricultural or food experts are thrust into the headlines . The very obscurity of agriculture and food , the almost quaintness with which the public treats the subject , is a mark of how successful our farmers and food processors have been . For most Canadians , food has become an afterthought , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . Over the past 20 years -- and compared with other countries -- funding for agricultural research and agricultural innovation has plummeted . We have enjoyed a " food dividend " analogous to the " peace dividend " that resulted from the dissolution of the Soviet Union . Much like how a long period of stability has led us to underfund defence , the success of the food system has led us to underfund agricultural innovation . <p> This time is over . The canary in the coal mine must be food price inflation and food insecurity . We live in a country where one in four of us are food insecure , and where our primary responses to this crisis are grassroots charities -- food banks -- that struggle to keep up with demand . <p> We need to recognize that , as well as addressing the military funding gap created by the peace dividend , we must increase funding to agricultural innovation that has been neglected owing to the " food dividend . " <p> We should declare Canada an agricultural country and set a massive target , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ production of food , and doubling the value of agriculture 's contribution to the economy . <p> The twin forces of global political uncertainty and climate change are challenging the food system like never before . <p> This is Canada 's moment . We can become the world 's most important food-producing nation . We can use this power to stand firm against the forces of chaos . But first , we will need the trading partners , the scientific engine , and the infrastructure to back this up . The time has come to develop and fund this vision so that we can indeed feed ourselves , and our partners , no matter what .

Gift this article to friends and family for free . <p> MILAN ( AP @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Siro stadium , classically trained dancers from La Scala 's academy mimicked Nordic walkers and figure skaters during a rehearsal Saturday for the opening number of the Milan Cortina Winter Olympicsopening ceremony , which will take place on Feb. 6 . <p> The young dancers are among some 1,200 volunteers who have been rehearsing since November in the tent large enough to mark the actual stage while Italian Serie A soccer wrapped up its final soccer matches before turning the iconic stadium into an Olympic venue . <p> " The preparation of the Olympic ceremony is a very complicated journey but also an exhilarating journey , because you get to meet all these volunteers , dance classes , normal people , ' ' opening ceremony creative director Marco Balich told The Associated Press during a rare behind-the-scenes tour . <p> Volunteers include Balich 's butcher , the head of his office and an 88-year-old widow . <p> " And all of them join in to create something for the nation , for the joy of being part of a huge event like the Olympics , ' ' said Balich , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ including the 2006 Turin opening ceremony . <p> Over the next two weeks , rehearsals will amp up to some nine hours a day -- all in pursuit of Olympic emotion for what is billed as the most viewed moment of the Games . Some 60,000 people are expected to attend the ceremony live in San Siro , including a U.S. delegation led by U.S. Vice President JD Vance , while millions around the world will watch on official broadcasters . <p> The theme of this year 's opening ceremony is " Harmony , ' ' an especially potent message as the world order is shaken and populations from Ukraine to Gaza to Iran are exposed to violence . <p> The concept of an Olympic Truce , originating in ancient Greece and revived by Olympic officials in the 1990s , is even more urgent this year , Balich said . The truce aims to promote peace and dialogue through sport by ceasing hostilities for a week before the Olympics and a week after the Paralympics , which close March 15 . Getting belligerents to cooperate is another matter . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ predominant , I think it 's very important for all of us to embrace the values that the Olympics represents , which is to compete respectfully and peacefully between all the countries and nations , summarized in the title ' Harmony , ' ' ' Balich said . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> Balich 's ceremony will highlight Italian excellence and creativity , including a nod to Milan 's role as a fashion capital , and eye-openers he wo n't reveal to preserve the surprise . <p> Others are prescribed by Olympic protocol . They include the unveiling of the Olympic rings , the parade of athletes and , in the final moment , the lighting of the Olympic cauldron . <p> This year there will be two cauldrons , inspired by Leonardo da Vinci 's geometric studies : one in Milan , at the Arco della Pace some four kilometers ( 2 1/2 miles ) from San Siro , and the other in Cortina , some five hours and 400 kilometers ( 250 miles ) away . <p> Director of ceremonies Maria Laura Iascone promised @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ flame over the final legs , given the extraordinary distances involved . <p> As the dual cauldron lighting illustrates , the 2026 Games are the most spread out in Olympic history . So that athletes even in far-flung venues near the Swiss and Austrian borders can participate , the Parade of Athletes will be beamed in from three other venues , including Cortina . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> " This event will bring a lot of this magic and images . We keep a balance between the protocol moments that will be , let 's say , serious , very precise , and also moments where emotion will be brought through the participation of key roles and people , ' ' Iascone said . <p> The rehearsal tent holds not only the mock stage , but also a huge wardrobe room with 1,400 costumes , some in bright broadcast-friendly Technicolor tones , and a corner for seamstresses and tailors to make final adjustments . <p> A sign on the door tells the performers who enter : " Your Happy Moment Starts Now ! An amateur dancer now living in Milan , he eagerly answered the casting call . <p> ' ' The last few moments before entering the stadium , it 's an experience , one of the best of my life , ever , ' ' Siadimas said . 
@@133702299 <p> Before Mark Carney spoke in Davos , French President Emmanuel Macron was on the same stage lamenting that the world is shifting to a place without rules , where the strongest dominate , pleading for a return to global co-operation . <p> After Mr. Carney 's speech , U.S. President Donald Trump talked about dominating other countries , claiming he forced Mr. Macron to double French drug prices and making threatening demands that Europe hand over Greenland . <p> In between , Mr. Carney 's words rang like a bell . <p> The old world order is over and it 's not coming back . He warned great powers that smaller countries will adapt , and hedge to cope with coercion . He warned other nations that complying with domineering hegemons would not keep them safe . He called for middle powers to band together to cope with the rapacious coercion of great powers . <p> The power of the speech was its clarity about the moment . From there , however , things got muddier . <p> The problem was n't really that Mr. Trump later revoked his @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Peace " overseeing Gaza reconstruction . Or U.S. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent 's devious suggestion that Albertans might join the U.S. and bring their oil . <p> It 's that the next steps into the new world order are full of impediments . <p> When the Prime Minister returned home to deliver a speech to Canadians in Quebec City , he did n't ring as clearly . And for all those leaders and power brokers going home from Switzerland , relieved that Mr. Trump backed off his threat of tariffs over Greenland , the notion of forging an alliance of middle powers might seem a distant hope . <p> In Quebec City , Mr. Carney could have followed his Davos diagnosis with straight talk about what it will mean to Canadians . But he did n't really do that . In Davos , he told global elites that a world of fortresses will make us all poorer but in Quebec City , he boasted that Canada will double its defence spending rather than counting the coming cost . <p> Most of that speech was a mildly interesting attempt to define @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ co-operation that implied a call for Canadian unity . But his domestic speech awkwardly wound in a hodgepodge of obligatory political messages about such things as affordability and crime . <p> Mr. Carney has already proposed bolstering economic independence by accelerating major projects and he promised a new AI strategy , but he did n't deliver a wake-up call for Canadian business leaders , policy makers and generals to rewire their thinking for the new world . Or how they might do it . <p> Still , the alarm that Mr. Carney had sounded in Davos was heard around the world . Yet his proposal for an alliance of middle powers , now presumably a fledgling Canadian initiative , is n't going to be easy to make a reality . Middle powers have almost never worked together to counter great-power hegemony . <p> " There have n't been a lot of historical examples , " said Queen 's University professor emeritus Kim Richard Nossal . Canada and especially Australia made unsuccessful proposals to establish a middle-power bloc after the Second World War , as the United Nations was being conceived @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . What now ? <p> The biggest impediment is that solidarity among smaller countries is hard to achieve because they usually have motivation to break ranks . <p> Canadian political scientist Stephen Nagy has argued that middle powers seeking to co-ordinate action against a great power face a structural impediment -- a version of the prisoner 's dilemma . Collectively , there are benefits to acting together but each has an interest in cutting their own deal . <p> Japan proposed a co-ordinated response to U.S. auto tariffs last year , but South Korea , which ca n't afford to lose U.S. military support , cut its own deal , Mr. Nagy , a professor at the International Christian University in Japan , noted in an article published in July . <p> More recently , Mr. Macron 's desire to threaten a quick European Union tariff response to Mr. Trump 's Greenland threats was slowed by Germany 's desire to maintain auto exports to the U.S. <p> Canadians should understand that conundrum . The economy relies on exports to the U.S. Its defence and security is inextricably reliant on the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to counter U.S. coercion , it implies a high risk of suffering through more punishing tactics . He has n't been so clear in spelling out what that might mean . 
@@133702581 <p> Canadian politics has always had its share of spin . What feels newer , and far more corrosive , is the growing comfort some politicians now have with simply abandoning the facts altogether , particularly when courts or public institutions are involved . Misrepresentation is no longer an occasional lapse or rhetorical flourish . It has become a strategy that trades accuracy for outrage and treats public trust as collateral damage . <p> Two social media posts earlier this month offered clear examples of how that strategy operates in practice . Different facts , different venues , but entirely the same modus operandi . In both cases , senior Conservative politicians took complex , legally constrained realities and recast them as scandal , grievance , and institutional failure . The aim was not to clarify or persuade , but to inflame , reinforcing a narrative that casts courts , universities , and institutions as enemies rather than essential parts of a functioning democracy . <p> The first example is one I know well because I was there . I represented a man who splashed red paint on Ottawa 's @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in Gaza . It was a serious crime , and he pleaded guilty . The sentencing process was lengthy and exacting , involving extensive submissions , detailed community and victim impact statements , and a careful examination of motive and case law . The sentencing judge ultimately delivered one of the most careful and principled decisions I have read in years . <p> The result was not some caricature of Liberal soft-on-crime leniency . The offender was sentenced to five months in jail and two years of probation . By the time the sentence was imposed , this first-time offender had already spent more than 150 days in actual prison and months under extraordinarily restrictive bail conditions , including house arrest , GPS monitoring , and effective exile from his children . The judge emphasized denunciation , deterrence , and the profound harm done to the Jewish community , while also explaining why the Crown had not met the very high burden of proving hate motivation beyond a reasonable doubt . The whole process was the rule of law doing precisely what it is designed to do . <p> Enter @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ A man defaces Canada 's Holocaust Monument with blood-red paint and faces no real jail time . Under the Liberals , antisemitism is tolerated , excused , and waved away for political convenience . " More than half a million people saw the post . It was also false . <p> Poilievre did not attend the sentencing . He did not read the decision . He appears not to have read any reporting beyond what fit neatly into a pre-written script . The offender faced real jail time and served it . He was detained at bail . He spent months in custody at the Ottawa-Carleton Detention Centre . He was released only after pleading guilty and while subject to strict bail terms , which he complied with . Disagree with the sentence if you like ; many do . But inventing facts to fuel grievance is something else entirely . For Poilievre , everything becomes evidence of Liberal rot , everything is softness , and any inconvenient facts are ignored . <p> It is entirely legitimate to criticize court decisions . I do it all the time . It @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ I do that all the time , too . What is unacceptable is for political leaders , who should be held to a higher standard than the rest of us , to deliberately misrepresent how the justice system works to score political points . That kind of misinformation corrodes trust in judges and courts and conditions the public to see every decision as partisan rather than principled . We do not need to look far south to see where that road leads . <p> If this pattern sounds familiar , it is because the same routine played out again , this time repackaged as a free-speech crisis . Conservative MP Garnett Genuis took to social media to announce that the student union had cancelled his event at York University in a " further attack on free speech . " The implication was obvious : politically motivated students were silencing conservative ideas . Genuis 's social media post travelled fast , as grievance narratives usually do . <p> Except that was not what happened . As CBC later reported , the event was not cancelled by the student union at all @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ booking rules for the proposed open-area town-hall format . Genuis was told he could book a closed space for that kind of event . He chose not to . In other words , no one silenced him . He cancelled himself . <p> That clarification arrived after the outrage had already done its work . Even fellow Conservatives swallowed the grievance whole . Michelle Rempel Garner publicly called for York University to be defunded . This is how political misinformation spreads : quickly , confidently , and with just enough plausibility to avoid immediate correction . Free speech was not under attack . Student unions were not censoring debate . But the narrative was politically useful , so accuracy became expendable . <p> Taken together , these incidents are not accidents or isolated missteps . They are features of a broader strategy . Modern Conservative messaging increasingly relies on manufacturing grievance through selective facts , exaggeration , and outright errors , confident that the truth will never travel as far or as fast as the original lie . <p> The justice system is imperfect . Universities are imperfect . Democracy @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ it depends on a shared commitment to reality . When politicians with power and privilege knowingly distort court decisions and invent free-speech panics to stoke resentment , they are not engaging in democratic debate . They are poisoning it . This grievance-first politics may generate outrage , donations , and viral posts . Still , it does so by eroding trust in the very institutions that allow a pluralistic society to function at all . At some point , we should stop pretending this is accidental . It is a strategy that prioritizes short-term political gain over the long-term health of Canadian democracy .

Champion recommends CTRL-F , a digital media literacy resource from the Canadian civic education charity CIVIX , to help guide students toward sources they can trust . <p> It also does n't hurt to have old fashioned news media around . The challenge is to get kids thinking beyond the immediate impact to themselves and consider the bigger picture . @@133865735 <h> Adekeye Adebajo Pax Americana : More continuity than change <p> A man holds a map of Greenland covered in the American flag crossed out with an X during a protest against Trump 's policy towards Greenland in front of the US consulate in Nuuk , Greenland , Saturday , January 17 , 2026 . <p> Adekeye Adebajo , professor and senior research fellow at the University of Pretoria 's Centre for the Advancement of Scholarship . <p> Government supporters rip an American flag in half during a protest in Caracas , Venezuela , Saturday , Jan. 3 , 2026 , after U.S. President Donald Trump announced that U.S. forces had captured President Nicol ? s Maduro and first lady Cilia Flores . <p> The United States ' abduction of Venezuelan President Nicol ? s Maduro and his subsequent transfer to New York to face drug-trafficking charges has inflicted grave damage on the United Nations-centred international legal system that the US largely built . To be sure , President Donald Trump is merely continuing a long history of US interventionism abroad . But whereas previous administrations often paid lip service @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the mask . By his own account , America 's mission in Venezuela is to seize control of the world 's largest oil reserves . <p> Ironically , the Monroe Doctrine that Trump has dusted off to justify expelling China ( which had been buying around 80 per cent of Venezuela 's oil exports ) from the Western Hemisphere was actually born of US military weakness . In seeking to bar European colonial powers from its self-declared backyard in the 1820s , the US initially had to rely on policing by Britain 's Royal Navy . <p> Not until US President Theodore Roosevelt 's 1904 " corollary " to the Monroe Doctrine did the US have the military capabilities to impose its will on the region , which it had already done by expelling Spain in 1898 . By then , Pax Americana had come to represent an expansionist project of " gunboat diplomacy " and " Yankee imperialism " in which the US annexed Hawaii , Guam , Samoa , and Puerto Rico , while occupying the Philippines and Cuba . Prior to that , the US had waged @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of its territory ( today 's California , Arizona , Colorado , Nevada , New Mexico , and Utah ) . <p> Similarly , during the Cold War , the US-backed vicious military caudillos in Brazil , Argentina , Chile , Uruguay , Paraguay , and Bolivia , while supporting right-wing death squads and insurgents in El Salvador , Guatemala , and Nicaragua in the name of anti-Communism . And further afield , in Iran , the US ( prodded by Britain ) toppled the democratically elected government of Mohammad Mossadegh in 1953 , then played a central role in the Belgian-assisted assassination of Congolese leader Patrice Lumumba in 1961 . <p> Two decades later , US President Ronald Reagan 's administration toppled a leftist military government in Grenada . His successor , George H.W. Bush , dispatched US Marines to arrest Panama 's military ruler , Manuel Noriega , on drug charges ( a striking prequel of Maduro 's abduction ) . <h> Headlines Delivered to Your Inbox <h> NOTORIOUS INVASION <p> In 1999 , US President Bill Clinton controversially bombed Serbia to the negotiating table without a UN @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ successor George W. Bush backed a corporate-led effort to depose Venezuela 's populist leader , Hugo Ch ? vez , but a mass uprising reversed the putsch . The Bush administration then launched its notorious invasion of Iraq ( a move opposed even by staunch allies like France and Germany ) on the false pretext that Saddam Hussein had weapons of mass destruction . Since no proper planning had been done for the aftermath , the intervention turned into a bloody quagmire , resulting in an estimated 461,000 Iraqi deaths by 2011 . <p> Finally , the 542 drone strikes launched during Barack Obama 's presidency killed an estimated 3,797 people , mainly in Yemen and Pakistan . In 2011 , Obama led the NATO bombing of Libya , and though that mission at least had been mandated by the UN Security Council to protect people from slaughter , it soon became a regime-change operation , culminating in the lynching of the country 's dictator . As Obama later explained , " we ... had to make sure that Muammar Qaddafi did n't stay there ... Qaddafi had more American @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ bin Laden . " <p> Obama destroyed Libya as surely as Bush destroyed Iraq . Again , no proper planning had been done for the aftermath of the intervention . The NATO mission left Libya in a state of anarchy . Over the following years , militants and arms spilled out across the already-volatile Sahel , a region that now accounts for over half of all terrorism-related deaths globally . <h> GLOBAL REACTION <p> The US has therefore historically embarked on a perennial quest for foreign enemies : from European imperialists to Latin American Marxists , Soviet Communists , Islamic jihadists , and Chinese mercantilists . What distinguishes Trump 's foreign policy from his predecessors ' is his crude and reckless use of military force , his inability to grasp the subtlety of soft power , and his wanton destruction of the multilateral institutions and alliances that have sustained America 's global hegemony for eight decades . Trump is also in a class of his own when it comes to flouting the rule of law within the US , engaging in shameless corruption and self-dealing and openly championing a nativist @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to Trump 's hubris in Venezuela shows that he has widened the world 's North-South split . Brazil , Mexico , Colombia , Cuba , China , and South Africa were particularly scathing in condemning the US action during an emergency session of the UN Security Council earlier this month . Even Russia , which has been waging an illegal war of aggression against Ukraine , had the gall to condemn the US with a straight face . <p> Meanwhile , Europe 's largely muted reaction has once again exposed its spinelessness in the face of a key ally who has gone rogue , just as it did when it condoned Israel 's massacres in Gaza . Yet such hypocrisy is self-defeating because Europe 's craven response to the situations in Gaza and Venezuela will diminish diplomatic support for Ukraine within the UN General Assembly . <p> And now Europe finds itself confronting US imperialism directly , in the form of Trump 's designs on Greenland . With American imperial aggression fully unmasked , Europeans , like people across the Western Hemisphere , may soon find themselves uttering a version @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ lament : " Poor Mexico , so far from God , so close to the US . " <p> Adekeye Adebajo , a professor and a senior research fellow at the University of Pretoria 's Centre for the Advancement of Scholarship . Article first published in Project Syndicate .

@@133930766 <p> Justin Ling is a staff columnist for the Star and an investigative journalist based in Montreal . Reach him by email : jling@thestar.ca <p> For a time , it was Kimberly Prost 's job to make sure only real terrorists appeared on the international sanctions list . <p> Being on this list means being cut off from much of the global financial system and barred from much of the Western world . If you found yourself on such a list in error two decades ago -- improperly accused of being an Al Qaeda member , say -- it was Prost 's job to help remove it . <p> Now Prost , a native of Winnipeg , finds herself on a similar register . Her name is on the United States ' sanction list , next to terrorists and war criminals . But Prost is neither : she 's a judge on the International Criminal Court , the tribunal responsible for trying those accused of committing crimes against humanity , war crimes and genocide . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> The Trump administration alleges Prost and seven of her ICC @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ or prosecute nationals of the United States or Israel , without the consent of either nation " -- and it has subjected them to penalties normally reserved for notorious criminals . The sanctions forbid any American company from providing any service to these eight judges , who are also barred from entering the U.S. <p> This is a naked attempt by President Donald Trump to destroy the crown jewel of our system of international law , and to shield Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu from legal accountability for his scorched-earth military operation in Gaza . <p> Unfortunately , it 's working . Yet although a Canadian judge is one of the targets of Trump 's campaign , Ottawa has yet to denounce the sanctions . " As the old saying goes , " Lloyd Axworthy , the former foreign affairs minister who helped establish the ICC , put it to me this week , " when you 're silent , you 're complicit . " <p> Canada has the power to actively counter these sanctions . We 've done it before . Why are n't we doing it now ? @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ genesis in the mid-20th century , when the Western Allies prosecuted top German and Japanese military and political figures for atrocities committed during the Second World War . But those ad hoc tribunals soon shut down , and a movement emerged calling for a more permanent , formalized process to hold alleged international criminals accountable . <p> In 1998 , the nations of the world assembled in Rome to hammer out plans for this new international court . Axworthy and his team , including Prost , were there to negotiate the final text and lobby other countries to vote for the plan . " I remember getting a call from Madeline Albright , " Axworthy told me . " She called me to commiserate that we were going to get beaten . " <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> Albright was the U.S. secretary of state , and while she personally supported the idea , the White House was publicly opposed to a permanent international court . <p> He was right , and it was n't close : 120 nations voted to adopt the Rome Statute , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> It has issued 61 arrest warrants to date , including for Russian President Vladimir Putin , Libyan dictator Moammar Gadhafi and Ugandan warlord Joseph Kony . To date , it has secured 13 convictions . <p> In 2024 , the ICC issued arrest warrants for Netanyahu , former Israeli defence minister Yoav Gallant and Hamas commander Mohammed Deif . The court says there 's reason to believe Deif authorized a slew of atrocities , including murder , rape and torture during the attacks on Israel on October 7 , 2023 . Netanyahu and Gallant are accused of having authorized systematic attacks on Palestinian civilians and used starvation as a weapon of war . <p> The ICC has grounds to pursue the case because Palestine is party to the Rome Statute , but Israel -- which has neither signed the statute nor recognized the Palestinian state -- calls it illegitimate . Netanyahu has gone even further , claiming the case is based on lies , accusing the lead prosecutor of corruption and alleging the court 's entire complement of judges is " motivated by an antisemitic hatred of Israel @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> America is n't party to the Rome Statute either , although it has co-operated with and supported the ICC in the past . But Trump , archenemy of all systems of international law and co-operation , sided with Israel . Calling the court an " extraordinary threat " to the U.S , Trump sanctioned the judges , the ICC 's chief prosecutor and a host of European Union officials . These sanctions are not only propagandistic attacks on international law ; they 're also having real impacts on the court 's ability to function . <p> " If you have assets in the United States , then they 're frozen , " Prost told the Irish Times . " You immediately lose your credit cards . It does n't matter where they were issued or by what bank . " <p> Nicolas Guillou , a French judge on the court , told Le Monde , " All my accounts with American companies , such as Amazon , Airbnb , PayPal and others , have been closed . " He likened living under the sanctions to " being sent back @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ global financial system means even non-U.S . companies may decide to heed them . <p> In other words , these sanctions are not merely an inconvenience . They can disrupt day-to-day functions of the court , affect the immigration status of judges ' relatives in America and prevent some judges from sending money transfers back to their families . Those who live under them experience " permanent anxiety and powerlessness , " Guillou said . Prost called the sanctions " an attack on the independence of the judiciary and the International Criminal Court 's independence as an institution . " <p> Last May , 375 diplomats , academics and legal experts -- including Axworthy -- signed a letter calling on Canada to issue a clear statement that " condemns any threats made against ICC personnel . " It has yet to do so . Foreign Affairs Minister Anita Anand has expressed confidence in Prost , and my Star colleague Ryan Tumilty reported in December that Anand had brought up the sanctions in meetings with U.S. Secretary of State Marco Rubio . <p> But when I asked Anand 's office for @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . A spokesperson wrote back to say Canada is " concerned " about the sanctions , but they would n't say whether the government planned to call for their removal . They added that Canada supports the " independence , impartiality and integrity " of the ICC but did n't indicate how . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> " We said nothing , " Axworthy told me . " And I just thought , That 's not a good sign . " <p> Our allies have been more forceful . French President Emmanuel Macron characterized Trump 's sanctions as " intimidation and coercion . " Slovenia has urged its fellow EU members to use their legal powers to nullify the American sanctions in Europe . Using a law called a blocking statute , the EU could instruct all European businesses to ignore the sanctions . <p> Canada has a similar legal power , and we 've even used it before : it 's called the Foreign Extraterritorial Measures Act . In 1997 , the federal government amended the law to override certain American sanctions against Cuba @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ island without fear of legal consequences in the U.S. <p> Parliament could expand the act to cover the ICC , its judges and all the European officials Trump has sanctioned , allowing them to access our banking sector and achieve some sense of normalcy . This would send a clear message that Canada will not allow a rogue state to frustrate international law . According to some legal experts , it 's also a clear use case for the act . Yet when I asked Anand 's office if the Liberals would consider invoking it , I received no reply . <p> It 's hardly surprising that we have n't invoked it so far . For much of 2025 , Carney and Trump shared something of a bromance . It was a relationship the prime minister 's team hoped to ride all the way to a new continental free-trade agreement -- but optimism about reaching a deal has since faded . <p> " Any attempt to butter up Trump is a lost cause , " Axworthy says . Indeed , a year of flattery has only earned us more of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> Axworthy takes solace in Carney 's recent comments in Davos , Switzerland , about the futility of negotiating with a hegemon and the " subordination " which comes with it . Instead , the prime minister told the World Economic Forum that if the liberal world bands together , " the power of legitimacy , integrity and rules will remain strong . " <p> Carney " talks about the importance of a world order of rules , " Axworthy said . " Well , who is going to enact them ? Who is going to make judgment on them ? Who 's going to enforce them , unless the institutions are there ? " <p> The enemies of the ICC are many -- Russia , China , North Korea and now America . " Empires are striking back , " Guillou said . Their leaders hate the court because it seeks to hold them accountable for their oppression and warmongering , and their crusade wo n't end with the ICC . Trump has set his sights on our entire global @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ resist today , the damage will be far greater tomorrow . <p> As Axworthy put it : " You do n't give up on something you 've spent close to a century building . Tim Walz and asked what he can do to help .
